Five report commands (`summary`, `top`, `daily`, `regions`, `near`) read a saved file, filter it, compute rows, and print CSV to standard output.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`; that file is the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Python 3.11+ (standard library only) | All application code. `urllib.request` for HTTP, `json`, `csv`, `decimal`, `datetime`, `math`, `re`. |\n| CLI | The `quakes` executable and the `cli` package. |\n| pytest | Test runner behind `bin/test.sh`; the only development dependency. |\n| USGS GeoJSON | Input format. |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh`. |\n\n## Project Layout\n\n| Path | Role |\n|---|---|\n| `quakes` | Executable launcher at the application root; calls `cli.main` and exits with its return value. |\n| `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` | Dependency manifest and lockfile. Runtime dependencies: none. |\n| `.venv` | Environment created by `uv venv`. |\n| `conftest.py` | Exposes the `cli_runner` pytest fixture from `cli.testing`. |\n| `cli/__init__.py` | `main(argv=None) -> int`, the command registry `COMMANDS`, exit-code mapping. |\n| `cli/errors.py` | `UsageError` (exit 2) and `OperationalError` (exit 1). |\n| `cli/options.py` | `parse_args(argv) -> Parsed`. |\n| `cli/formatting.py` | `format_time`, `format_mag`, `format_mean`, `format_decimal`, `render_csv`, `write_csv`. |\n| `cli/feed.py` | `read_feed(path)`, `extract_events(features)`, `Event`, `filter_events(events, min_mag)`. |\n| `cli/reports.py` | One pure function per report, plus `haversine_km`. |\n| `cli/fetch.py` | `build_url`, `http_get`, the fetch command. |\n| `cli/testing.py` | `cli_runner(*args) -> (exit_code, stdout, stderr)`. |\n| `tests/` | Application pytest suite. |\n| `bin/test.sh` | POSIX sh wrapper that runs the pytest suite. |\n| `sources/` | Read-only scoring assets; never modified and never read by application code. |\n\n## Boundaries and Interfaces\n\nReading the file, filtering, computing each report, and formatting are separate functions:\n\n1. `read_feed(path) -> list[dict]` validates and returns raw features.\n2. `extract_events(features) -> list[Event]` normalizes and drops null-magnitude events.\n3. `filter_events(events, min_mag) -> list[Event]` applies `--min-mag`.\n4. A report function maps events (and its options) to a list of rows, each row a list of already-formatted strings.\n5. `write_csv(rows, stream=None)` writes rows with the `csv` module.\n\n`Parsed` (returned by `parse_args`) is a dataclass with fields `command: str`, `min_mag: float | None`, `n: int`, `lat: float | None`, `lon: float | None`, `radius: float | None`, `start: str | None`, `end: str | None`, `out: str | None`, `file: str | None`. `n` defaults to `10`. `min_mag` is `None` for report commands when omitted and `2.5` for `fetch` when omitted.\n\nCommand handlers are registered in `cli.COMMANDS: dict[str, Callable[[Parsed], str]]`. A handler returns the complete standard-output text for the command. `main` writes that text to standard output only after the handler returns, so a failing command leaves standard output empty.\n\n## Exit Codes and Channels\n\n| Code | Cause |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Success, including a report with no matching events. |\n| 1 | `OperationalError`: unreadable file, not a FeatureCollection, fetch network/HTTP/body failure. |\n| 2 | `UsageError`: any malformed invocation. |\n\nA failing command writes nothing to standard output and exactly one diagnostic line to standard error. Standard error is never compared by the grader.\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Half-up rounding uses `decimal.Decimal(repr(x))` quantized with `ROUND_HALF_UP`; built-in `round()` is not used.\n- Text ordering is by byte value (plain Python string comparison on code points, with UTF-8 output).\n- Tests stub `cli.fetch.http_get`; no test opens a network connection.\n- No report row, count, or distance is hardcoded; every value comes from the file read.\n\n## Module Ownership\n\nNo persistent store, configuration file, or external service other than the USGS HTTP endpoint exists. File I/O is owned by `cli.feed` (reading) and `cli.fetch` (writing the fetched file). HTTP is owned by `cli.fetch.http_get`. No `DATABASE.md` applies.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC arch-project-files ===\nIntent: The project root carries a dependency manifest, lockfile, tests directory, and a conftest exposing the cli_runner fixture.\nSuite: scoped\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nassert Path(\"pyproject.toml\").is_file()\nassert Path(\"uv.lock\").is_file()\nassert Path(\"tests\").is_dir()\nconftest = Path(\"conftest.py\")\nassert conftest.is_file()\nassert \"cli_runner\" in conftest.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n=== END AC arch-project-files ===\n\n=== AC arch-stdlib-only ===\nIntent: The project declares no runtime dependencies and requires Python 3.11 or newer.\n\nimport sys\nimport tomllib\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ndata = tomllib.loads(Path(\"pyproject.toml\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\nproject = data[\"project\"]\nassert project.get(\"dependencies\", []) == []\nassert sys.version_info >= (3, 11)\n=== END AC arch-stdlib-only ===\n\n=== AC arch-pytest-available ===\nIntent: The pytest runner is importable in the project environment.\n\nimport importlib.util\n\nassert importlib.util.find_spec(\"pytest\") is not None\n=== END AC arch-pytest-available ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Do not modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute any file under `sources/`.\n- Do not read `sources/run.sh` or the snapshot from application code, and do not hardcode a report row, a count, or a distance.\n- Standard library only at runtime; no third-party runtime dependency.\n- Tests never open a network connection.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CliFoundation.md ===\n# FEATURE: CLI Foundation\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Publishes the cli package, its main entry point, the cli_runner test helper, and the executable quakes launcher. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | cli.main, cli.testing.cli_runner, ./quakes |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nThis foundation stands up the command-line entry point everything else registers into. It delivers the `cli` package, `main(argv=None) -> int`, the in-process `cli_runner` helper, and an executable file named `quakes` at the application root.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int` — when `argv` is `None` it uses `sys.argv[1:]`. Returns the process exit code and never raises for `UsageError` or `OperationalError`.\n- `cli.COMMANDS` — registry mapping command name to a handler `Callable[[Parsed], str]` returning the full standard-output text.\n- `cli.testing.cli_runner(*args) -> tuple[int, str, str]` — runs `main` in-process with redirected stdout and stderr and returns `(exit_code, stdout, stderr)`.\n- `conftest.py` exposes `cli_runner` as a pytest fixture.\n- `./quakes` — executable file (mode with the execute bit set, `#!/usr/bin/env python3`) that puts its own directory on `sys.path`, calls `cli.main()`, and exits with the returned value.\n\nDispatch is a skeleton in this story; later stories register commands and argument parsing.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC clifound-main-returns-int ===\nIntent: main accepts an argv list and returns an integer exit code.\n\nfrom cli import main\n\ncode = main([])\nassert isinstance(code, int)\n=== END AC clifound-main-returns-int ===\n\n=== AC clifound-runner-shape ===\nIntent: cli_runner returns an exit code, stdout text, and stderr text, and agrees with main.\n\nfrom cli import main\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nargs = [\"no-such-command-xyz\"]\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(*args)\nassert isinstance(code, int)\nassert isinstance(out, str)\nassert isinstance(err, str)\nassert code == main(list(args))\n=== END AC clifound-runner-shape ===\n\n=== AC clifound-registry ===\nIntent: The command registry is a dict that main consults for dispatch.\n\nimport cli\n\nassert isinstance(cli.COMMANDS, dict)\n=== END AC clifound-registry ===\n\n=== AC clifound-launcher ===\nIntent: The quakes launcher at the application root is executable and exits with the same code main returns.\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nfrom cli import main\n\nassert os.path.isfile(\"quakes\")\nassert os.access(\"quakes\", os.X_OK)\nargv = []\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./quakes\", *argv], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == main(list(argv))\n=== END AC clifound-launcher ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- The launcher contains no report logic; it only delegates to `cli.main`.\n- Diagnostics go to standard error only.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Options.md ===\n# FEATURE: Options\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Parses and validates the command, its options, and the file argument, raising a usage error for any malformed invocation. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CliFoundation.md |\n| Provides    | parse_args |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`cli.options.parse_args(argv) -> Parsed` turns the argument list into a validated `Parsed` record or raises `cli.errors.UsageError`. `UsageError` is defined in `cli/errors.py` (see `FEATURE-ExitCodes.md`); this story creates the module with both exception classes if it does not exist yet. The dispatcher maps `UsageError` to exit code 2.\n\n## Grammar\n\n```text\nquakes fetch --start <YYYY-MM-DD> --end <YYYY-MM-DD> [--min-mag <m>] --out <file>\nquakes summary [--min-mag <m>] <file>\nquakes top [--min-mag <m>] [--n <count>] <file>\nquakes daily [--min-mag <m>] <file>\nquakes regions [--min-mag <m>] <file>\nquakes near --lat <deg> --lon <deg> --radius <km> [--min-mag <m>] <file>\n```\n\n- The command is the first argument. Options follow in any order; the file (for report commands) is last.\n- Each command accepts only its listed options; any other option is a `UsageError`.\n- `<m>`, `<deg>`, `<km>` match `-?[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?` and a value may begin with `-`. Anything else is malformed.\n- `<count>` is a positive integer (digits only, greater than zero), default `10`.\n- `--lat` is within -90 to 90, `--lon` within -180 to 180, `--radius` greater than zero. `near` requires all three.\n- `fetch` requires `--start`, `--end`, `--out`; dates match `YYYY-MM-DD`. `fetch` takes no positional file. `--min-mag` defaults to `2.5` for `fetch`; for report commands it is `None` when omitted.\n- A missing command, unknown command, missing option value, missing file, extra positional argument, or repeated option is a `UsageError` (decision Q-001).\n- `parse_args` does not touch the file system.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC options-valid-summary ===\nIntent: A valid summary invocation parses into the expected command, filter, and file.\n\nfrom cli.options import parse_args\n\npath = \"quakes-file.geojson\"\nparsed = parse_args([\"summary\", \"--min-mag\", \"5.5\", path])\nassert parsed.command == \"summary\"\nassert parsed.min_mag == 5.5\nassert parsed.file == path\n=== END AC options-valid-summary ===\n\n=== AC options-defaults ===\nIntent: top defaults n to 10, reports default min_mag to None, and fetch defaults min_mag to 2.5.\n\nfrom cli.options import parse_args\n\npath = \"quakes-file.geojson\"\ntop = parse_args([\"top\", path])\nassert top.n == 10\nassert top.min_mag is None\nfetch = parse_args([\"fetch\", \"--start\", \"2025-01-01\", \"--end\", \"2025-01-08\", \"--out\", path])\nassert fetch.min_mag == 2.5\n=== END AC options-defaults ===\n\n=== AC options-any-order ===\nIntent: Options may appear in any order and produce the same parsed values.\n\nfrom cli.options import parse_args\n\npath = \"quakes-file.geojson\"\na = parse_args([\"top\", \"--n\", \"3\", \"--min-mag\", \"5.1\", path])\nb = parse_args([\"top\", \"--min-mag\", \"5.1\", \"--n\", \"3\", path])\nassert a.n == b.n == 3\nassert a.min_mag == b.min_mag == 5.1\nassert a.file == b.file == path\n=== END AC options-any-order parsed.radius == 0.5\nnegative = parse_args([\"summary\", \"--min-mag\", \"-1\", path])\nassert negative.min_mag == -1.0\n=== END AC options-near-valid ===\n\n=== AC options-fetch-valid ===\nIntent: \"--start\", start, \"--end\", end, \"--min-mag\", \"5\", \"--out\", out])\nassert parsed.command == \"fetch\"\nassert parsed.start == start\nassert parsed.end == end\nassert parsed.out == out\nassert parsed.min_mag == 5.0\n=== END AC options-fetch-valid ===\n\n=== AC options-usage-errors ===\nIntent: Every malformed invocation raises UsageError.\n\nfrom cli.errors import UsageError\nfrom ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Usage errors never print to standard output; the parser raises `UsageError` rather than using argparse's default error behavior.\n- An option Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Maps usage and operational failures to exit codes 2 and 1 with empty standard output and one standard-error line. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Options.md |\n| Provides    | UsageError, OperationalError, exit code mapping |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`cli/errors.py` defines `UsageError` (exit 2) and `OperationalError` (exit 1), both subclasses of a common `QuakesError(Exception)` carrying a one-line message. `cli.main` parses arguments, looks up the handler in `cli.COMMANDS`, and runs it.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Success, including a report with no matching events, returns `0` after writing the handler's returned text to standard output.\n- `UsageError` returns `2`; `OperationalError` returns `1`.\n- On either failure `main` writes nothing to standard output and exactly one diagnostic line (ending in a single newline, no embedded newline) to standard error.\n- Handlers return their complete output text and write nothing themselves, so a failure leaves standard output empty.\n- An unexpected exception is not mapped; only the two named errors are.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC exit-usage-code ===\nIntent: A usage error returns exit code 2 with empty stdout and a single stderr line.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"frobnicate\", \"quakes-file.geojson\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\nassert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n=== END AC exit-usage-code ===\n\n=== AC exit-operational-code ===\nIntent: An OperationalError raised by a handler returns exit code 1 with empty stdout and a single stderr line.\n\nimport cli\nfrom cli.errors import OperationalError\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ndef failing(parsed):\n    raise OperationalError(\"cannot read the file\")\n\noriginal = cli.COMMANDS.get(\"summary\")\ncli.COMMANDS[\"summary\"] = failing\ntry:\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(\"summary\", \"quakes-file.geojson\")\nfinally:\n    if original is Output is UTF-8 and non-ASCII place names are written unescaped.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC fmt-time ===\nIntent: format_time yields a UTC timestamp truncated to whole seconds whose first BEGIN ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE.md ===\n# ARCHITECTURE: Quakes\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Module layout, boundaries, and technical decisions for the standard-library-only `quakes` command-line earthquake reporter. |\n| Depends On  | None |\n| Provides    | project tree, pytest runner |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`quakes` is a single-process Python 3.11+ command-line tool at the application root. `fetch` downloads a USGS FDSN GeoJSON feed to a file. Five report commands (`summary`, `top`, `daily`, `regions`, `near`) read a saved file, filter it, compute rows, and print CSV to standard output.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`; that file is the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Python 3.11+ (standard library only) | All application code. `urllib.request` for HTTP, `json`, `csv`, `decimal`, `datetime`, `math`, `re`. |\n| CLI | The `quakes` executable and the `cli` package. |\n| pytest | Test runner behind `bin/test.sh`; the only development dependency. |\n| USGS GeoJSON | Input format. |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh`. |\n\n## Project Layout\n\n| Path | Role |\n|---|---|\n| `quakes` | Executable launcher at the application root; calls `cli.main` and exits with its return value. |\n| `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` | Dependency manifest and lockfile. Runtime dependencies: none. |\n| `.venv` | Environment created by `uv venv`. |\n| `conftest.py` | Exposes the `cli_runner` pytest fixture from `cli.testing`. |\n| `cli/__init__.py` | `main(argv=None) -> int`, the command registry `COMMANDS`, exit-code mapping. |\n| `cli/errors.py` | `UsageError` (exit 2) and `OperationalError` (exit 1). |\n| `cli/options.py` | `parse_args(argv) -> Parsed`. |\n| `cli/formatting.py` | `format_time`, `format_mag`, `format_mean`, `format_decimal`, `render_csv`, `write_csv`. |\n| `cli/feed.py` | `read_feed(path)`, `extract_events(features)`, `Event`, `filter_events(events, min_mag)`. |\n| `cli/reports.py` | One pure function per report, plus `haversine_km`. |\n| `cli/fetch.py` | `build_url`, `http_get`, the fetch command. |\n| `cli/testing.py` | `cli_runner(*args) -> (exit_code, stdout, stderr)`. |\n| `tests/` | Application pytest suite. |\n| `bin/test.sh` | POSIX sh wrapper that runs the pytest suite. |\n| `sources/` | Read-only scoring assets; never modified and never read by application code. |\n\n## Boundaries and Interfaces\n\nReading the file, filtering, computing each report, and formatting are separate functions:\n\n1. `read_feed(path) -> list[dict]` validates and returns raw features.\n2. `extract_events(features) -> list[Event]` normalizes and drops null-magnitude events.\n3. `filter_events(events, min_mag) -> list[Event]` applies `--min-mag`.\n4. A report function maps events (and its options) to a list of rows, each row a list of already-formatted strings.\n5. `write_csv(rows, stream=None)` writes rows with the `csv` module.\n\n`Parsed` (returned by `parse_args`) is a dataclass with fields `command: str`, `min_mag: float | None`, `n: int`, `lat: float | None`, `lon: float | None`, `radius: float | None`, `start: str | None`, `end: str | None`, `out: str | None`, `file: str | None`. `n` defaults to `10`. `min_mag` is `None` for report commands when omitted and `2.5` for `fetch` when omitted.\n\nCommand handlers are registered in `cli.COMMANDS: dict[str, Callable[[Parsed], str]]`. A handler returns the complete standard-output text for the command. `main` writes that text to standard output only after the handler returns, so a failing command leaves standard output empty.\n\n## Exit Codes and Channels\n\n| Code | Cause |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Success, including a report with no matching events. |\n| 1 | `OperationalError`: unreadable file, not a FeatureCollection, fetch network/HTTP/body failure. |\n| 2 | `UsageError`: any malformed invocation. |\n\nA failing command writes nothing to standard output and exactly one diagnostic line to standard error. Standard error is never compared by the grader.\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Half-up rounding uses `decimal.Decimal(repr(x))` quantized with `ROUND_HALF_UP`; built-in `round()` is not used.\n- Text ordering is by byte value (plain Python string comparison on code points, with UTF-8 output).\n- Tests stub `cli.fetch.http_get`; no test opens a network connection.\n- No report row, count, or distance is hardcoded; every value comes from the file read.\n\n## Module Ownership\n\nNo persistent store, configuration file, or external service other than the USGS HTTP endpoint exists. File I/O is owned by `cli.feed` (reading) and `cli.fetch` (writing the fetched file). HTTP is owned by `cli.fetch.http_get`. No `DATABASE.md` applies.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC arch-project-files ===\nIntent: The project root carries a dependency manifest, lockfile, tests directory, and a conftest exposing the cli_runner fixture.\nSuite: scoped\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nassert Path(\"pyproject.toml\").is_file()\nassert Path(\"uv.lock\").is_file()\nassert Path(\"tests\").is_dir()\nconftest = Path(\"conftest.py\")\nassert conftest.is_file()\nassert \"cli_runner\" in conftest.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n=== END AC arch-project-files ===\n\n=== AC arch-stdlib-only ===\nIntent: The project declares no runtime dependencies and requires Python 3.11 or newer.\n\nimport sys\nimport tomllib\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ndata = tomllib.loads(Path(\"pyproject.toml\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\nproject = data[\"project\"]\nassert project.get(\"dependencies\", []) == []\nassert sys.version_info >= (3, 11)\n=== END AC arch-stdlib-only ===\n\n=== AC arch-pytest-available ===\nIntent: The pytest runner is importable in the project environment.\n\nimport importlib.util\n\nassert importlib.util.find_spec(\"pytest\") is not None\n=== END AC arch-pytest-available ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Do not modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute any file under `sources/`.\n- Do not read `sources/run.sh` or the snapshot from application code, and do not hardcode a report row, a count, or a distance.\n- Standard library only at runtime; no third-party runtime dependency.\n- Tests never open a network connection.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CliFoundation.md ===\n# FEATURE: CLI Foundation\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Publishes the cli package, its main entry point, the cli_runner test helper, and the executable quakes launcher. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | cli.main, cli.testing.cli_runner, ./quakes |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nThis foundation stands up the command-line entry point everything else registers into. It delivers the `cli` package, `main(argv=None) -> int`, the in-process `cli_runner` helper, and an executable file named `quakes` at the application root.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int` — when `argv` is `None` it uses `sys.argv[1:]`. Returns the process exit code and never raises for `UsageError` or `OperationalError`.\n- `cli.COMMANDS` — registry mapping command name to a handler `Callable[[Parsed], str]` returning the full standard-output text.\n- `cli.testing.cli_runner(*args) -> tuple[int, str, str]` — runs `main` in-process with redirected stdout and stderr and returns `(exit_code, stdout, stderr)`.\n- `conftest.py` exposes `cli_runner` as a pytest fixture.\n- `./quakes` — executable file (mode with the execute bit set, `#!/usr/bin/env python3`) that puts its own directory on `sys.path`, calls `cli.main()`, and exits with the returned value.\n\nDispatch is a skeleton in this story; later stories register commands and argument parsing.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC clifound-main-returns-int ===\nIntent: main accepts an argv list and returns an integer exit code.\n\nfrom cli import main\n\ncode = main([])\nassert isinstance(code, int)\n=== END AC clifound-main-returns-int ===\n\n=== AC clifound-runner-shape ===\nIntent: cli_runner returns an exit code, stdout text, and stderr text, and agrees with main.\n\nfrom cli import main\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nargs = [\"no-such-command-xyz\"]\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(*args)\nassert isinstance(code, int)\nassert isinstance(out, str)\nassert isinstance(err, str)\nassert code == main(list(args))\n=== END AC clifound-runner-shape ===\n\n=== AC clifound-registry ===\nIntent: The command registry is a dict that main consults for dispatch.\n\nimport cli\n\nassert isinstance(cli.COMMANDS, dict)\n=== END AC clifound-registry ===\n\n=== AC clifound-launcher ===\nIntent: The quakes launcher at the application root is executable and exits with the same code main returns.\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nfrom cli import main\n\nassert os.path.isfile(\"quakes\")\nassert os.access(\"quakes\", os.X_OK)\nargv = []\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./quakes\", *argv], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == main(list(argv))\n=== END AC clifound-launcher ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- The launcher contains no report logic; it only delegates to `cli.main`.\n- Diagnostics go to standard error only.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Options.md ===\n# FEATURE: Options\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Parses and validates the command, its options, and the file argument, raising a usage error for any malformed invocation. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CliFoundation.md |\n| Provides    | parse_args |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`cli.options.parse_args(argv) -> Parsed` turns the argument list into a validated `Parsed` record or raises `cli.errors.UsageError`. `UsageError` is defined in `cli/errors.py` (see `FEATURE-ExitCodes.md`); this story creates the module with both exception classes if it does not exist yet. The dispatcher maps `UsageError` to exit code 2.\n\n## Grammar\n\n```text\nquakes fetch --start <YYYY-MM-DD> --end <YYYY-MM-DD> [--min-mag <m>] --out <file>\nquakes summary [--min-mag <m>] <file>\nquakes top [--min-mag <m>] [--n <count>] <file>\nquakes daily [--min-mag <m>] <file>\nquakes regions [--min-mag <m>] <file>\nquakes near --lat <deg> --lon <deg> --radius <km> [--min-mag <m>] <file>\n```\n\n- The command is the first argument. Options follow in any order; the file (for report commands) is last.\n- Each command accepts only its listed options; any other option is a `UsageError`.\n- `<m>`, `<deg>`, `<km>` match `-?[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?` and a value may begin with `-`. Anything else is malformed.\n- `<count>` is a positive integer (digits only, greater than zero), default `10`.\n- `--lat` is within -90 to 90, `--lon` within -180 to 180, `--radius` greater than zero. `near` requires all three.\n- `fetch` requires `--start`, `--end`, `--out`; dates match `YYYY-MM-DD`. `fetch` takes no positional file. `--min-mag` defaults to `2.5` for `fetch`; for report commands it is `None` when omitted.\n- A missing command, unknown command, missing option value, missing file, extra positional argument, or repeated option is a `UsageError` (decision Q-001).\n- `parse_args` does not touch the file system.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC options-valid-summary ===\nIntent: A valid summary invocation parses into the expected command, filter, and file.\n\nfrom cli.options import parse_args\n\npath = \"quakes-file.geojson\"\nparsed = parse_args([\"summary\", \"--min-mag\", \"5.5\", path])\nassert parsed.command == \"summary\"\nassert parsed.min_mag == 5.5\nassert parsed.file == path\n=== END AC options-valid-summary ===\n\n=== AC options-defaults ===\nIntent: top defaults n to 10, reports default min_mag to None, and fetch defaults min_mag to the command does not list is always a usage error.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ExitCodes.md ===\n# FEATURE: Exit Codes\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Maps usage and operational failures to exit codes 2 and 1 with empty standard output and one standard-error line. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Options.md |\n| Provides    | UsageError, OperationalError, exit code mapping |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`cli/errors.py` defines `UsageError` (exit 2) and `OperationalError` (exit 1), both subclasses of a common `QuakesError(Exception)` carrying a one-line message. `cli.main` parses arguments, looks up the handler in `cli.COMMANDS`, and runs it.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Success, including a report with no matching events, returns `0` after writing the handler's returned text to standard output.\n- `UsageError` returns `2`; `OperationalError` returns `1`.\n- On either failure `main` writes nothing to standard output and exactly one diagnostic line (ending in a single newline, no embedded newline) to standard error.\n- Handlers return their complete output text and write nothing themselves, so a failure leaves standard output empty.\n- An unexpected exception is not mapped; only the two named errors are.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC exit-usage-code ===\nIntent: A usage error returns exit code 2 with empty stdout and a single stderr line.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"frobnicate\", \"quakes-file.geojson\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\nassert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n=== END AC exit-usage-code ===\n\n=== AC exit-operational-code ===\nIntent: An OperationalError raised by a handler returns exit code 1 with empty stdout and a single stderr line.\n\nimport cli\nfrom cli.errors import OperationalError\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ndef failing(parsed):\n    raise OperationalError(\"cannot read the file\")\n\noriginal = cli.COMMANDS.get(\"summary\")\ncli.COMMANDS[\"summary\"] = failing\ntry:\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(\"summary\", \"quakes-file.geojson\")\nfinally:\n    if original is None:\n        del cli.COMMANDS[\"summary\"]\n    else:\n        cli.COMMANDS[\"summary\"] = original\nassert code == 1\nassert out == \"\"\nassert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n=== END AC exit-operational-code ===\n\n=== AC exit-success-code ===\nIntent: A handler that returns text yields exit 0 and that exact text on stdout, and an empty result is still success.\n\nimport cli\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ntext = \"header\\nrow\\n\"\noriginal = cli.COMMANDS.get(\"daily\")\ncli.COMMANDS[\"daily\"] = lambda parsed: text\ntry:\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(\"daily\", \"quakes-file.geojson\")\n    cli.COMMANDS[\"daily\"] = lambda parsed: \"\"\n    empty_code, empty_out, _ = cli_runner(\"daily\", \"quakes-file.geojson\")\nfinally:\n    if original is None:\n        del cli.COMMANDS[\"daily\"]\n    else:\n        cli.COMMANDS[\"daily\"] = original\nassert code == 0\nassert out == text\nassert empty_code == 0\nassert empty_out == \"\"\n=== END AC exit-success-code ===\n\n=== AC exit-error-classes ===\nIntent: UsageError and OperationalError are distinct exception classes.\n\nfrom cli.errors import OperationalError, UsageError\n\nassert issubclass(UsageError, Exception)\nassert issubclass(OperationalError, Exception)\nassert not issubclass(UsageError, OperationalError)\nassert not issubclass(OperationalError, UsageError)\n=== END AC exit-error-classes ===\n\n=== AC exit-launcher-status ===\nIntent: The quakes executable surfaces exit 2 for a usage error with empty stdout.\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./quakes\", \"frobnicate\", \"quakes-file.geojson\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == 2\nassert result.stdout == \"\"\n=== END AC exit-launcher-status ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A failing command never writes to standard output.\n- Standard error carries exactly one diagnostic line on failure; its wording is not part of the contract.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Formatting.md ===\n# FEATURE: Formatting\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Formats UTC times, magnitudes, depths, distances, and CSV output for every report. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | format_time, format_mag, format_mean, format_decimal, write_csv |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`cli/formatting.py` holds the pure formatting functions every report uses.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `format_time(ms: int | float) -> str` — epoch milliseconds to UTC `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, truncated (floored) to whole seconds. The date is the first 10 characters.\n- `format_mag(x) -> str` — one decimal place (`5.0`, `7.1`).\n- `format_mean(x) -> str` — two decimal places, rounded half up.\n- `format_decimal(x) -> str` — one decimal place, rounded half up (used for `depth_km` and `distance_km`).\n- `render_csv(rows) -> str` — rows (lists of strings) rendered with Python's `csv` module, `lineterminator=\"\\n\"`, so a field containing a comma is quoted.\n- `write_csv(rows, stream=None)` — writes `render_csv(rows)` to `stream`; when `stream` is `None` it writes UTF-8 to standard output (the underlying binary buffer when present, otherwise `sys.stdout.write`).\n\nRounding uses `decimal.Decimal(repr(x))` quantized with `ROUND_HALF_UP` (decision Q-002); built-in `round()` is not used. Output is UTF-8 and non-ASCII place names are written unescaped.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC fmt-time ===\nIntent: format_time yields a UTC timestamp truncated to whole seconds whose first 10 characters are the date.\n\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\n\nfrom cli.formatting import format_time\n\nseconds = 1735706358\nms = seconds * 1000 + 999\ntext = format_time(ms)\nparsed = datetime.strptime(text, \"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\").replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)\nassert int(parsed.timestamp()) == seconds\nassert len(text) == 20\nassert text[:10] == parsed.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d\")\nassert format_time(seconds * 1000) == text\n=== END AC fmt-time ===\n\n=== AC fmt-magnitude ===\nIntent: Magnitudes are written with exactly one decimal place.\n\nfrom cli.formatting import format_mag\n\nassert format_mag(5) == \"5.0\"\nassert format_mag(7.1) == \"7.1\"\nassert format_mag(5.0) == \"5.0\"\n=== END AC fmt-magnitude ===\n\n=== AC fmt-mean-half-up ===\nIntent: mean_mag has two decimals and rounds exact halves up.\n\nfrom cli.formatting import format_mean\n\nassert format_mean(5.3) == \"5.30\"\nassert format_mean(5.125) == \"5.13\"\nassert format_mean(5.135) == \"5.14\"\nassert format_mean(5) == \"5.00\"\n=== END AC fmt-mean-half-up ===\n\n=== AC fmt-decimal-half-up ===\nIntent: depth and distance have one decimal and round exact halves up.\n\nfrom cli.formatting import format_decimal\n\nassert format_decimal(0.25) == \"0.3\"\nassert format_decimal(10) == \"10.0\"\nassert format_decimal(216.25) == \"216.3\"\nassert format_decimal(46) == \"46.0\"\n=== END AC fmt-decimal-half-up ===\n\n=== AC fmt-csv-roundtrip ===\nIntent: render_csv round-trips fields containing commas and non-ASCII characters through the csv parser with \\n line endings.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\n\nfrom cli.formatting import render_csv\n\nplace = \"\\u0100wash, Ethiopia\"\nrows = [[\"time\", \"place\"], [\"2025-01-01T00:00:00Z\", place], [\"a\", \"plain\"]]\ntext = render_csv(rows)\nparsed = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(text, newline=\"\")))\nassert parsed == rows\nassert text.count(\"\\n\") == len(rows)\nassert text.endswith(\"\\n\")\n=== END AC fmt-csv-roundtrip ===\n\n=== AC fmt-write-csv ===\nIntent: write_csv writes the rendered CSV to the given stream and to standard output by default.\n\nimport contextlib\nimport io\n\nfrom cli.formatting import render_csv, write_csv\n\nrows = [[\"h1\", \"h2\"], [\"x\", \"y, z\"]]\nstream = io.StringIO()\nwrite_csv(rows, stream)\nassert stream.getvalue() == render_csv(rows)\n\ncaptured = io.StringIO()\nwith contextlib.redirect_stdout(captured):\n    write_csv(rows)\nassert captured.getvalue() == render_csv(rows)\n=== END AC fmt-write-csv ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- CSV is always produced with Python's `csv` module, never by string concatenation.\n- Rounding never uses built-in `round()`.\n=== END ARTIFACT
